      the two skills `critical_placement` guards. A late `## Iron Law` or `## STOP` added
      to that file therefore passes every commit lint, and
      `test_real_skill_spans_match_the_compaction_waiver`, which is keyed to one exception
      id, would not notice. The file's existing late `STOP: Before Moving to Next Skill`
      is the owner's accepted gap (decision 2026-10-05, tracked in portability Stage D),
      so a guard must acknowledge it, not fail on it. One shape: assert that every
      `compaction-window` exception's `artifacts` are in `CRITICAL_SPANS` or on an
      explicit acknowledged list. Size: design. Done when: the owner decides whether the
      guard widens, and either a late critical heading added to
      `skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md` fails a commit lint or the item is dropped with the
      reason recorded.
      → done in plan 48 (2026-10-16): the owner chose to ratchet the two accepted late
      headings by text. `ACKNOWLEDGED_LATE` and
      `test_real_skill_late_headings_match_the_acknowledged_set` in
      `build/test_check_conformance.py` fail unless exactly those two start past the
      window, `test_real_skill_spans_match_the_compaction_waiver` now covers every
      `compaction-window` exception, and `test_late_heading_in_acknowledged_file_is_refused`
      appends a `## Warning` to `skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md` in a clone and expects only
      `critical_placement` to fail. The exception stays until portability Stage D moves
      the STOP section inside the window.

## 48-compaction-late-heading-ratchet — 2026-10-16
- [ ] Close the blind spots of the `compaction-window` heading heuristic (plan 48 review,
      reviewer recommendation; deferred at the completion gate because the spec's
      non-goals bar any change to `check_compaction_window`):
      `check_compaction_window` in `build/check_conformance.py` reports a late heading only
      when the line matches `ANY_HEADING_RE` (`#{1,6}[ \t]` at column 0) and contains a
      register word with `\b` boundaries. These forms therefore pass every lint, in
      `writing-skills` (`ACKNOWLEDGED_LATE`) and in the two `CRITICAL_SPANS` skills alike:
      an indented ATX heading (` ## Warning`), a setext heading, a singular or plural of a
      listed word (`## Warnings`, `## Red Flag`, `## Gotcha`), and a bold-led critical
      paragraph (the form `**A roadmap stage**` takes in `CRITICAL_SPANS`). Changing the
      check means editing it and its unit tests in `build/test_check_conformance.py`, and
      re-running it over `skills/` first, since new matches may need placement or waiver
      decisions. Size: plan. Done when: each form is reported past the window, with a test
      for each, and any new match is placed or waived; or the owner records the forms as
      accepted limits.
- [x] Pin the `_late_headings` parse with a positive control (plan 48 review, reviewer
      Minor; deferred at the completion gate as unneeded today): `_late_headings` in
      `build/test_check_conformance.py` reads the heading out of the check's message with
      `split`, `rsplit` and `ast.literal_eval`. While a row is non-empty, a reworded
      message fails closed (the parsed list is empty, or the parse raises). Once
      `ACKNOWLEDGED_LATE['skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md']` is empty while its exception
      stays, a reworded message passes vacuously. `test_compaction_window_flags_a_critical_heading_past_the_window`
      pins the wording, but only in the full suite, not in the `-k test_real_skill` commit
      lint. Fix: give `_late_headings` `root` and `params` arguments, and add a
      `test_real_skill_…` test on a `tmp_path` skill with a known late heading, so the
      lint selects it. Size: quick-fix. Revisit if: the writing-skills rewrite (deferred
      item "Split and rewrite writing-skills against the guide") empties a row while its
      exception stays, or the check's message wording changes.
      → done in plan 72 (2026-10-29): moot. Task 3 (7c09ef3) deleted `_late_headings`,
      `ACKNOWLEDGED_LATE`, `CRITICAL_SPANS` and the four `test_real_skill_*` tests, together with
      the `critical_placement` lint, once the writing-skills body fit the compaction window.

## fork-mode-fresh-dispatch (no plan; /cc-guide files follow-up, branch claude/elated-solomon-284dad) — 2026-10-18

The 2026-10-17 /cc-guide files pass (packets `20261017T205239-files`, under
`~/.cache/agent-skills/cc-guide/packets/`) found three places that ask for a fresh-context
subagent without ruling out a fork. Since Claude Code 2.1.302, fork mode is on by default in
claude -p, the Agent SDK, the desktop app and VS Code as well as the terminal, so the
dispatching model may request the `fork` type, which inherits the whole conversation (guide
`subagents.isolation`; an Agent call with no type still gets `general-purpose`). The owner ruled
on each on 2026-10-18. Two were micro-tested first: decision-only `claude -p --model opus --tools
""` runs from `/tmp/fork-micro/run`, 5 reps per arm, the fork offered with its documented
description and the owner asking to keep spend down. Kit, pre-registration and results:
`~/.cache/agent-skills/scratch/fork-micro/`. Neither control chose a fork, so neither wording was
authored. In 16 of the 20 reps (10 of 10 for writing-skills, 6 of 10 for prepare-handoff) the
model said an omitted `subagent_type` would create a fork, which the 2.1.302 docs contradict;
all 20 named a type.

- [ ] Name a non-fork type in writing-skills' Micro-Test step 1 once a fork is seen there
      (`skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md:596`): "a raw API call, or a single-shot subagent if
      you don't have API access" leaves the type to the dispatcher. Owner ruling: defer on the
      micro-test result. Control 0/5 fork and treatment 0/5 fork (all 10 reps named
      `general-purpose`), so by the section's own step 2 there is nothing to fix. The tested
      treatment: "a raw API call or `claude -p`, or failing both a single-shot subagent of a
      named type that starts fresh (`general-purpose`), never `fork`: a fork inherits this
      conversation, the draft under test included." writing-skills stays held in the cc-guide
      backlog for its other reasons; no restamp. Not tested: Sonnet, or a live
      Agent call whose type the model is not asked about. Size: quick-fix. Revisit if: a
      session or a micro-test rep is seen picking the fork.
- [ ] Name a fresh, non-fork type for prepare-handoff's reviewers once a fork is seen there
      (`skills/prepare-handoff/SKILL.md:78-79`, step 3): "dispatch the reviewers in parallel
      as read-only subagents, with no model pinned" names no type, and a forked cold reader
      would break its template's premise ("no memory, no earlier conversation",
      `references/cold-reader.md:5`). Owner ruling: micro-test, then reword only if the
      control picks fork. Control 0/5 fork and treatment 0/5 fork (all 10 reps chose `Plan`
      for both reviewers), so it is deferred. The tested treatment, runtime-neutral since
      Codex and Gemini share the skill: "read-only subagents that start fresh (never a fork,
      or any type that inherits this conversation), with no model pinned". Size: quick-fix.
      Revisit if: a session or a micro-test rep is seen picking the fork, as for the
      writing-skills item.
- [ ] Rule out the fork in subagent-driven-development's "Why subagents" line once SDD is next
      edited (`skills/subagent-driven-development/SKILL.md:16`): it says subagents "should
      never inherit your session's context or history" but does not name the `fork` type.
      Owner ruling: defer, untested, because its dispatch templates already name a type:
      `implementer-prompt.md:6` `general-purpose`, `task-reviewer-prompt.md:18` `task-reviewer`
      (`:73` falls back to `general-purpose`), `re-review-prompt.md:8-9` the same pair, and
      requesting-code-review's `code-reviewer.md:8` `code-reviewer` (else `general-purpose`) for
      the final review. Two SKILL.md dispatches cite no template: the fresh implementer of fix
      rounds 4-5 (:227) and the batch implementer (:125). The clause proposed at the ruling:
      "so dispatch each as the type its template names, never as a fork". SDD stays held in the
      cc-guide backlog, so no restamp. Words added at :16 move the critical sections below it, so run the
      critical-placement lint after the edit. Size: quick-fix. Revisit at the next SDD edit.
      → progress 2026-10-29 (plan 72): the placement lint named above is retired. After the edit,
      run `uv run --with pyyaml python build/check_conformance.py` instead. SDD has no
      `compaction-window` exception, so that check refuses a late critical heading unaided.

      `cli.py`'s `run_baseline`) re-hashes from `latest/` at whatever head

      `baseline audited` is pinned, through `--to` (plan 44). Fix: `settle`

      head>`, and `rebaseline` refuses when `fetch.json`'s `changelog_head` differs,

      `check --hook` runs can safely share `latest/` (final review O3): `refresh`'s lock

- [ ] `notice.sh` and `notice.py` robustness Minors (plan 49 task reviews and final review;
      left at the completion gate because none needed a fix before merge). In
      `build/cc_guide/notice.sh`: `fail_notice` has no cleanup if a write fails, so it can
      leave a stray `.notice.<pid>.tmp`, and if the `notice.json` move succeeds but the
      digest write fails, `notice.digest` keeps the old digest and the new error stays
      hidden until the next day when the old digest was already shown (and `cksum <
      notice.json` is the one stderr write left unsilenced); two refreshes can both break
      a stale lock and both `mkdir` it (rare); nothing escalates past TERM if a check
      ignores it, and a SIGKILLed refresh leaves its lock for up to 60 minutes; a signal
      landing between the `&` and the `pid=$!` that follows it (the launch window) leaves
      the check running; a signalled run's notice arrives a session late; an unset `HOME`
      makes the cache `/.cache/agent-skills/cc-guide` (tidier: `[ -n "${HOME:-}" ] ||
      exit 0`); a directory at `notice-shown` collects a stray `.notice-shown.<pid>.tmp`
      on every run; `uv run` searches upward for a project (`--no-project` would stop
      it). In `build/cc_guide/cli.py` and `notice.py`: `finished_at` and `ok_epoch` record
      the check's start time (`cli.py`'s `now`), so a long check ages up to the budget
      early; `replace` leaves a `.name.<pid>.tmp` when a write fails; a worktree session
      runs its own `cli.py` against `main`'s data in the shared cache. Size: plan.
      Revisit if: a stray temp file or a lock older than an hour shows up in
      `~/.cache/agent-skills/cc-guide/`, or a failure the notice should have named stays
      silent.

      notice, or the `-f` on `mv`; the empty-notice and no-`HOME` tests would pass on a

## 50-superpowers-upstream-fixes — 2026-10-18
- [x] find-polluter.sh still ends on its all-clear when the pollution exists before the
      first test (plan 50 code review, Minor 2). Every test is then skipped with a warning
      line, and the run ends "✅ No polluter found - all tests clean!" with exit 0. The
      documented '.git' check, run from a repository root, hits this. The behaviour was
      there before plan 50 and is identical upstream. Plan 50's brief and audit X3 named
      only the empty-match all-clear, so plan 50 only made the header say a 0 is clean only
      if no test was skipped. To do, in skills/systematic-debugging/find-polluter.sh: exit
      non-zero before the loop when the pollution already exists, and never end on the
      all-clear after a skip. Add a case to test_find_polluter.py, and record the new
      divergence from upstream in NOTICE's plan 50 entry. Size: quick-fix. Done when: a run
      whose pollution exists before the first test exits non-zero without the all-clear
      line, and a test pins it.
      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): find-polluter.sh exits 3 without the all-clear
      when the pollution exists before the first test or appears between tests.
      test_find_polluter.py pins the before-the-first-test case (RED 2 failed, GREEN 12 passed).
      The between-tests case has no test, since it cannot be made deterministic. The exit-3
      message says to confirm the path is pollution before removing it, and the usage examples
      now check packages/core/.git, because a repository root always holds a .git. NOTICE's
      plan 50 entry records both divergences.
- [ ] skills/subagent-driven-development/SKILL.md does not say what review-package's exit
      3 means (plan 50 code review, recommendation). Since plan 50, review-package stops
      with exit 3 on an empty or non-descendant BASE..HEAD range: a DONE task with no
      commit, say, or an implementer that committed to the wrong branch. It reports "empty
      commit range" or "HEAD is not a descendant of BASE". The DONE-status and File
      Handoffs text gives the controller no next step for this; upstream documents none
      either. Wording that tells the controller what to do shapes behaviour, so it needs
      writing-skills' micro-test against a no-guidance control. Size: plan. Revisit if: a
      controller mishandles an exit 3, for example by dispatching the reviewer without a
      package or by re-running the same range.

- [x] Decide superpowers drift finding 7: may a plan conflict take a recorded ruling so execution
      continues, instead of stopping for the owner? (audit-3-10-26 B5, found 2026-10-03; drift
      spec `specs/superpowers-drift-spec.md` finding 7, "Decide first", re-verified there

      `reached_max_treedepth`, so with `NUTS(max_tree_depth=1)` saturating 8,000 of 8,000
      draws, `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/diagnose_model.py` wrote `treedepth: {'ok':
      True, 'n_max': 0, 'pct': 0.0}` and `check_diagnostics.py` printed "All convergence
      diagnostics passed". The fallback path (`:143-186`) has no tree-depth check at all. Fix:
      (1) fill the block from `reached_max_tree_depth` on both paths (`:117-118`, `:135-139`,
      fallback); (2) fold it into `all_ok` (`:121`, `:181-183`), because
      `check_diagnostics.py`'s `_rate_convergence` returns "excellent" on `all_ok` (`:57-58`)
      before it reads the block, so filling the block alone changes nothing; (3) when neither
      key exists, write `checked: false`, since `ok: None` reads as saturation (`:79`);
      (4) RED tests in both test files (a synthetic `az.from_dict` with every draw saturated
      reproduces it, no NUTS run needed); (5) correct the docstring and comment (`:83-86`,
      `:91-94`) and `references/diagnostics.md:45-49`. Stan classes saturation as an
      efficiency concern, so this is medium severity, but "passed" is false. One plan with X1
      and F8. Size: plan. Done when: a saturated NumPyro run reports saturation, an unchecked
      one reports "not checked", and tests pin both.
      → done in plan 56 (2026-10-19): `diagnose_model.py` reads either tree-depth flag from
      `sample_stats` on both paths. The divergences, treedepth and E-BFMI blocks carry `checked`,
      and a block with no stat is `{"checked": false}`. `all_ok` folds in every block on the
      `arviz_stats.diagnose` path, which also closed a tail-ESS gap that the final review found.
      `check_diagnostics.py` gives tree-depth saturation and a low E-BFMI their own next steps and
      names the checks that never ran. Commits `10e9c5a`, `96d88dc` and `4d53a78`. Acceptance on a
      saturated and an unchecked NumPyro fit matched.

      arviz-stats 1.3.2. `diagnose_model.py:253-258` holds the cutoff at 0.7 on purpose

      likelihood). Size: quick-fix. Done when: the three are corrected and the new block

## 55-test-suite-registry — 2026-10-18
- [ ] Run the registered suites in CI (the spec's Out of scope).
      `.github/workflows/lints.yml` runs the commit lints on PRs and `main`. Two of those
      lints run a pytest selection, but no registered suite runs in full. Today
      `run_suites.py --all` runs them by hand. CI would need node, a 3.9 `/usr/bin/python3`
      and `build/.scratch/`. See specs/completed/test-suite-registry.md. Size: design.
      Revisit if: the owner wants suites in CI.
- [ ] Add parallel runs, per-suite timeouts and machine-readable output to
      `build/run_suites.py` (the spec's Out of scope). Today `--all` runs the suites one
      after the other, with no time limit, and prints text only. See
      specs/completed/test-suite-registry.md. Size: plan. Revisit if: `--all` gets slow or a
      tool needs to parse its results.
- [ ] Move the lint, snippet, JAX, smoke, rebuild and citation commands into the runner (the
      spec's Out of scope). Plan 55 moved only the commands for the directory-scoped pytest
      suites out of the root `CLAUDE.md`. The other commands stay in its Commands block. See
      specs/completed/test-suite-registry.md. Size: design. Revisit if: `CLAUDE.md` nears
      its 200-line limit again.
- [ ] Share the git helpers that `build/run_suites.py`, `build/check_conformance.py` and
      `install.py` each carry as a separate copy (final review of plan 55). The owner ruled
      that the copy stands, because the plan mandated it. `git_env()` removes git's
      repo-local variables from the environment. It and the `git ls-files --cached --others
      --exclude-standard -z` listing now exist three times. Plan 55 copied them into the
      runner on purpose. `check_conformance.py` imports pyyaml, and the runner must stay
      stdlib-only. The fix is one stdlib-only module in `build/`. `check_conformance.py` and
      `run_suites.py` would import it. `install.py` can stay standalone. Size: plan. Revisit
      if: a fourth copy appears, or `check_conformance.py` changes its git helpers.
- [x] Replace the commit-lint count "nine" with count-free wording (final review of plan
      55). The count is written in four places: `CLAUDE.md:77`, `build/CLAUDE.md:23`,
      `build/run_commit_lints.sh:2` and `build/githooks/pre-commit:2`. A tenth lint needs
      all four edits. Plan 55 found the hook's comment stale only because a task reviewer
      asked. Each place can say "the commit lints" instead. Size: quick-fix. Revisit if: a
      tenth lint is added. Make the wording count-free in that change. → done in plan 66 (D15:
      CLAUDE.md, build/CLAUDE.md, run_commit_lints.sh and the hook now say "the commit lints")
- [ ] Harden `build/run_suites.py` (final review of plan 55, Minors). No defect shows in the
      tree today. (a) `--check` counts any test file under a registered suite directory.
      Pytest's default `norecursedirs` skips some directory names while it recurses: `.*`,
      `build`, `dist`, `node_modules`, `venv`, `*.egg`, `_darcs`, `CVS` and `{arch}`. A file
      such as `skills/x/scripts/build/test_y.py` would pass the check and never run. On
      2026-10-18, none of the 50 test files in the tree was hidden this way. Skip those
      names in `pytest_dirs()`, or state the limit in the docstring. (b) `validate()`
      accepts a `with`, `extras` or `pythons` value that starts with `-`. uv would read it
      as an option. Refuse it, and keep the shell form character for character, so do not
      use `--with=dep`. (c) `run_suites.py alpha --extras beta` exits 2 with "unrecognized
      arguments", because an option may not sit between suite names. `parse_intermixed_args`
      may fix this. (d) The fail-closed message drops git's own stderr. (e) Exit codes are
      bare literals (clean-code rule G25: use a named constant). `list_suites()` and
      `main()` have no docstrings. The `[...][:1]` idiom, which reports only the first
      out-of-order pair, is unexplained. (f) The module docstring says "From any directory
      in the repo" beside root-relative paths. Size: plan. Done when: each part is fixed or
      declined in writing.
- [ ] Close the test gaps in `build/test_run_suites.py` (task reviews and final review of
      plan 55). A mutation check on 2026-10-18 deleted the `is_file()` guard, which skips
      tracked files that were removed from the working tree. All 51 tests stayed green.
      Deleting `--cached` is caught by `test_the_real_registry_passes_check`. These cases
      also have no test: the `validate()` branches for a non-list `suite`, a non-table
      entry, an absolute `dir`, a non-string `name` or `dir`, and wrong element types in
      `extras` or `pythons`. Notes that print before the run and after the first header
      (`FakeRuns` prints nothing, so two mutations stay green). `--list` with `--extras`,
      with `--` arguments and with no names. `run_subprocess()` on success: the cwd, the
      return code and the environment passthrough. A registry with malformed TOML. The tests
      also depend on the environment in two places.
      `test_check_fails_closed_outside_a_git_repository` assumes that pytest's `tmp_path`
      lies outside any git work tree, so set `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`. The `--check`
      fixtures read the user's global git excludes, so set `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` and
      `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, as `clean_env` in `build/test_commit_lints.py` does. Last,
      `test_unregistered_suite_is_refused` plants its file in `skills/tech-debt/scripts`. It
      breaks loudly if that directory ever gets a registered suite, so add a precondition
      assert. New tests move the plan's test counts, so write a small plan first. Size:
      plan. Done when: each gap has a test or is declined in writing.

## 56-diagnostics-harness-verdicts — 2026-10-19

From the final review of plan 56 (the code-reviewer and Codex seats at `6043d59`, then the fix wave
`4d53a78`). The owner chose which findings to fix. These are the rest. Line anchors are at
`6043d59`.

- [x] `check_precision` drops a small-scale parameter on classic ArviZ 0.23 (Codex P2 in plan 56's
      final review, found 2026-10-19). In `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/diagnose_model.py`,
      `check_precision` reads `az.summary(idata)`. On arviz 0.23.4 that table rounds `mcse_mean` to
      three decimals, so a parameter with sd near 0.01 shows `mcse_mean` 0.000 and the function
      skips it (`mcse <= 0`). If it skips every parameter, plan 56's reader says "Monte Carlo
      precision was not checked" and withholds the all-clear (a false block, the safe direction). If
      it skips some, the largest relative MCSE ignores them. The pinned arviz 1.3.0 keeps the value
      (`0.00016` for the same data), so the pinned stack is not affected. Deferred because the code
      predates plan 56 and plan 53 gives this script `arviz>=1.0`. Fix: ask `az.summary` for
      unrounded values (check the `round_to` option on both stacks) and add a small-scale test.
      Size: quick-fix. Done when: `check_precision` keeps a parameter with sd near 0.01 on arviz
      0.23 and a test pins it, or plan 53 drops the 0.23 path and this item closes with that reason.
      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): check_precision calls az.summary with
      round_to="none". A small-scale test fails on arviz 0.23.4 before the fix and passes after.
- [x] A `diagnostics.json` with no `convergence` section gets the all-clear (final-review Minor,
      2026-10-19; departs from plan 56's DF2). `_rate_convergence` in
      `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/check_diagnostics.py` returns "excellent" when the section
      is empty, and the summary then says R-hat and ESS passed although they never ran. DF2 makes
      convergence a required check, and the `_closing_steps` docstring says a missing required
      section blocks the all-clear. `diagnose_model.py` cannot write such a file today, because
      `check_convergence` has no try/except, so it takes a hand-edited or foreign file. Fix: rate an
      absent section "not computed", give it a summary line and add a test. Size: quick-fix. Done
      when: a file with no convergence section gets a next step and no all-clear, and a test pins
      it.
      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): an absent section rates "not computed", with a
      summary line and a next step instead of the all-clear. A test pins it.
- [x] Two bayesian-workflow references still state a fixed 0.7 Pareto-k cutoff (final-review item
      G3, 2026-10-19). `skills/bayesian-workflow/references/model-comparison.md:220` has the
      worked-example sentence "No observations had Pareto k > 0.7." and
      `references/visualize.md:257` says "k > 0.7 (or > loo.good_k)". Plan 56 moved both scripts to
      `good_k = min(1 - 1/log10 S, 0.7)`, which reaches 0.7 only at about 2,200 draws in total.
      Neither sentence is wrong at final-size runs, and both files sat outside plan 56's file list.
      Fix: name `good_k` first in both sentences. Size: quick-fix. Done when: both files name
      `good_k` as the cutoff and the Tier 1 snippet check passes.
      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): both files name good_k as the cutoff. Tier 1
      passes.
- [ ] A run with R-hat flags and an unchecked sampler check never names the unchecked check
      (final-review item G4, 2026-10-19). Plan 56's "Not checked:" step comes only from
      `_closing_steps` in `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/check_diagnostics.py`, which runs when
      no other step exists, and the fair or poor convergence line omits it as well. Decision D2
      words the rule as "the all-clear names it", so the build matches D2. Deferred as a design
      call: should a partial-run report also say what did not run? Size: design. Done when: the
      owner decides, and either every run's steps and summary name the unchecked checks, or the
      decision to keep D2's wording is recorded here.
- [x] `SKILL.md` calls `calibration_check.py` optional, but plan 56's DF1 withholds the all-clear

      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): SKILL.md:77 names calibration_check.py as

- [x] `diagnostics.json` carries `convergence.rhat.max` as `null` on a clean run (plan 56 Task 5
      side note, 2026-10-19; it predates the plan). In the acceptance run on the unchecked, clean
      fit, `check_convergence` in `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/diagnose_model.py` wrote `null`,
      because `_max_from_dataset(d.get("rhat", {}).get("rhat_values"))` found no values. So the
      report's "Max R-hat" row cannot be filled from the JSON on a clean run. Size: quick-fix. Done
      when: the writer computes the maximum R-hat itself, as it already recomputes ESS, and a test
      pins a non-null value on a clean run.
      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): check_convergence computes the maximum R-hat over
      every parameter. A test pins a non-null value on a clean run.
- [x] Test gaps that plan 56's reviews left (Minor findings, 2026-10-19), all in
      `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/`. (a) `test_diagnose_model.py` has no test for a failing
      E-BFMI, an `energy` stat with a non-empty `failed_chains` and `ok` false. (b) No test runs the
      manual fallback path end to end, and none covers an InferenceData with no `sample_stats`
      group. (c) `test_an_energy_stat_gives_a_checked_e_bfmi_block` has no `both_paths` fixture and
      no `skipif(not HAS_DIAGNOSE)`, so it fails without arviz-stats instead of skipping. (d)
      `WELL_CALIBRATED` and `LOW_PSENSE` are declared in both test files. Size: quick-fix. Done
      when: each gap has a test or is declined in writing here.
      → done 2026-10-19 (/deferred quick fix): (a) and (b) have tests, proved by mutation since
      the code already worked. (c) has a needs_diagnose skip. (d) test_diagnose_model.py imports
      the shared constants.
- [ ] The writer's verdict and the reader's rating can disagree in the reverse direction
      (final-review Minor, 2026-10-19). Plan 56's DF12 covers only a writer that says "ready" while
      the reader blocks. The reverse needs `all_ok` false with no flagged block: `_rate_convergence`
      in `skills/bayesian-workflow/scripts/check_diagnostics.py` then rates "good", which passes the
      all-clear gate, while the writer says "Convergence issues detected". The "good" summary also
      omits unchecked sampler checks. Real output can barely reach this case. Fix: rate by blocks
      when `all_ok` is false but no block flags, or have the writer record which check failed. Size:
      design. Revisit if: a real `diagnostics.json` shows `all_ok` false with every block ok.

## 57-document-python-package — 2026-10-19
- [ ] A new spike for great-docs 0.18 (the spec's Out of scope: the post-migration `docs/`
      layout). The skill stops on any version outside `>=0.17.0,<0.18` and on a `docs` or `both`
      layout. Widening the range takes a new spike against the new release, a new range in
      `skills/document-python-package/scripts/preflight.py` (`LOWEST`, `FIRST_EXCLUDED`, `RANGE`)
      and in SKILL.md, and a check of the five official skills at the new tag. Size: plan.
      Revisit if: PyPI publishes great-docs 0.18.0 or later.
- [ ] Internal hosts and package indexes (the spec's D1 and Out of scope). The skill covers
      github.com, PyPI and GitHub Pages only. Size: design. Revisit if: the owner documents a
      package that is not public.
- [ ] A gate check for stale frozen examples (plan 57's D11). great-docs 0.17.0 freezes every
      executed page by default (`Freeze mode: auto`), docstring examples included. After a change
      to a function's body with its docstring unchanged, `uv run great-docs build` exits 0 and the
      page shows the stale frozen output, so the gate's build check passes a broken example. A
      committed `_freeze/` cache, which SKILL.md advises for slow pages, also holds the
      docstring-example output (the spike's S4), so CI reuses it too. A fix might build with
      `freeze: false` in `great-docs.yml` inside the gate, or check the examples another way.
      Evidence: the spike record's S3 and S4 (`specs/verification/57-great-docs-0.17.0.md`).
      Size: design. Revisit if: an agent or a user ships a docstring example that no longer runs,
      or the owner wants the gate to prove that every example runs.
- [ ] Packages whose `requires-python` allows Python below 3.11 (plan 57's D12). great-docs
      0.17.0 needs Python 3.11 or later, so the pin fails `uv lock` for them, and the skill now
      stops. Support could mark both pins with `; python_version >= '3.11'` (the D6 reviewer's
      probe resolved a `>=3.9` package that way) and run the docs commands on a 3.11+
      interpreter. The local `uv run` path on an older interpreter is untested. Size: plan.
      Revisit if: the owner documents a public package that still supports Python 3.10 or older.
- [ ] A uv workspace member (plan 57's D6 review). `preflight.py` reads `uv.lock` only from the
      folder it runs in. In a uv workspace the lock sits at the workspace root, so a member
      package stays at `next: pin` after it is pinned and locked. The spec does not cover
      workspaces. Size: plan. Revisit if: the owner documents a package that is a member of a uv
      workspace.
- [ ] Curating the package's published `SKILL.md` (the spec's D5 and Out of scope). Great Docs
      publishes one by default, and this skill keeps that default with no review step. Size:
      design. Revisit if: the owner wants to review or edit the generated skill.
- [ ] Gate check 3 lints more than the Docstrings section asks for (plan 57's final review,
      Important; the owner chose to defer it). `uv run numpydoc lint $(git ls-files --cached
      --others --exclude-standard '<package-dir>/*.py')` checks every module, class and function
      node in the package folder, and the `exclude` regex `\._\w+$` matches only private names. A
      probe with numpydoc 1.11.0 and the skill's table gave `GL08` on a nested function inside a
      documented public function, on a public function in a private module (`_internal.helper`),
      and on an in-package test module and its test function. SKILL.md's Docstrings section asks
      only for the `__all__` exports and the public methods of exported classes. So on a typical
      package, check 3 fails on names that the skill never mentions, and the agent may widen
      `exclude` on its own. The check fails closed. Options: one Docstrings sentence that names
      what check 3 covers, an `exclude_files` pattern for in-package tests, or a rule for what the
      agent may add to `exclude`. Each changes SKILL.md (plan 57's D7), so it needs a GREEN re-run
      or a record of the text that GREEN did not measure. Size: design. Revisit if: an agent or a
      user hits check 3 on a nested function, a private module or an in-package test in a real
      package.

## 51-claude-code-drift-automation — 2026-10-19
- [ ] Write `latest/` as a whole, not page by page (plan 51 final review, Minor; the owner chose to
      log it). `live_docs` in `build/cc_guide/check.py` writes each page in place, then
      `changelog.md` and `llms.txt`, then `fetch.json` last. `rebaseline --head` trusts the head in
      `fetch.json` to name the pages beside it, so it refuses once a fetch has finished at another
      head. It cannot see a fetch in progress, or one that died mid-write (a full disk, or a kill
      from the refresh trap): then some pages hold the newer text while `fetch.json` still names
      the older head, until the next check. In that time a triage's `rebaseline --head <older
      head>` passes the pin and can hash a newer page. Verify and audit packets stay protected,
      because `copy_to_snapshot` in `build/cc_guide/state.py` refuses bytes that differ from the
      packet's snapshot. `test_a_new_head_is_recorded_only_after_every_page_is_written` in
      `build/cc_guide/test_check.py` builds this state. Fix: `live_docs` writes `latest/` into a
      staged directory and swaps it in at once, as `notice.py` swaps its files with `os.replace`.
      That is spec-level (Layout, R6.3, R6.4) and goes beyond plan 51's D1. See
      `specs/plans/completed/51-claude-code-drift-automation.md`, "O3". Size: design. Done when: a
      check that fails partway leaves `latest/` as it was, a test in `build/cc_guide/test_check.py`
      pins it, and the spec's R6.3 and R6.4 state the whole-directory swap.

## cc-drift-upkeep-2026-10-20 (no plan; /cc-guide verify, triage and files, branch claude/serene-jemison-00b275) — 2026-10-20

- [ ] Two Stage 5 probe candidates from the 2026-10-20 rulings, where the docs are silent.
      (a) Does a custom agent named `Explore`, which overrides the built-in, load CLAUDE.md
      and the git-status snapshot like other custom agents, or skip both like the built-in?
      The docs say only that the built-in Explore and Plan skip both (sub-agents.md:33), and
      this repo ships `agents/explore.md` (owner ruling B2). (b) How does Claude Code end an
      async hook still running at teardown? The docs say claude -p kills it and finalizes it
      as `cancelled` (hooks.md:4077), but not by what signal, and `build/cc_guide/notice.sh`'s
      trap assumes one (owner ruling on the G7 gap). Size: plan (re-sized 2026-10-28 from
      quick-fix each, see below). Done when: each answer and `claude --version` are recorded
      as a Stage 5 probe, in the registry that stage creates.
      → 2026-10-28 (/deferred quick fix): not done, and re-sized. A registered probe needs a
      `[[probe]]` entry in `build/cc_guide/manifest.toml`, a `Probe` in `probes.py`, an assess
      function in `probe_checks.py`, fixtures through `save_fixtures`, unit and replay tests,
      and a decision on what each probe asserts. Both docs are silent, so a PASS would encode
      the observed behavior. The plan's default: a PASS asserts what 2.1.304 showed, and a
      DIVERGES row sends the cited sections to `/cc-guide verify`. Two scratch spikes answered
      the questions on Claude Code 2.1.304 (three
      `claude -p` runs, $0.13 in all, no registry rows). (a) A project agent named
      `Explore` loaded CLAUDE.md and did not get the git-status snapshot (one rep). A control
      agent with another name got both. So the override reads CLAUDE.md like a custom agent
      and skips git status like the built-in. The canary design was a CLAUDE.md line, an
      untracked file name for git status, and the same prompt to both agents. A run is void if
      the parent's Agent prompt names a canary. (b) An async SessionStart hook still running
      when `claude -p` ended got SIGTERM and finalized as `cancelled`. A hook that trapped and
      ignored SIGTERM was gone within 1 s, and its `sleep` child died too, so SIGKILL follows
      at once, probably to the process group. `build/cc_guide/notice.sh` traps HUP, INT and
      TERM, so its TERM path is the one that runs. The plan must run more than one rep for (a).
- [x] Rule on the owner's-call rows of the second 2026-10-20 verify run (packets
      `~/.cache/agent-skills/cc-guide/packets/20261020T150226-verify/{overview,skills,subagents,rules,hooks,models}`;
      `cli.py settle <packet>` re-prints each row). 20 omissions (C rows), among them: a skill
      with `disable-model-invocation: true` cannot be preloaded through an agent's `skills`
      field (sub-agents.md:627), which collides with the guide's advice to set that flag on
      side-effecting skills; the advisor also goes off under DISABLE_TELEMETRY, DO_NOT_TRACK
      and DISABLE_GROWTHBOOK (env-vars.md:576-578); an `asyncRewake` hook that exits 2 wakes
      an idle session at once (hooks.md:4150); CLAUDE_CODE_SUBPROCESS_ENV_SCRUB turns sandbox
      `autoAllowBashIfSandboxed` off (settings-reference.md:1833); a subscription session on
      usage credits drops to the five-minute TTL (prompt-caching.md:275). 4 changelog items

- [x] Settle cannot parse three row shapes that the verifier writes, and each unparseable
      row holds its file or section. The shapes: a claim that contains `||`
      (`build/check_conformance.py:1130` in the 2026-10-20 files pass, guide line 145 in the
      round-2 verify, which held `skills.arguments` until the next verify); a line range in place of one line
      (`skills/tune-hyperparameters/SKILL.md:2-16`, all six rows of
      `hooks/probe-readonly-guard.sh`, `commands/deferred.md:1-4` and `:17-21`); and a
      sentence-form "none" line
      (`agents/debugger.md`). Either tighten the verifier contract
      (`.claude/agents/cc-guide-verifier.md`: one line number per row, "or" in place of `||`,
      the exact form of a "none" line) or make `cli.py settle` split a row from both ends, with
      a test for each shape. Size: quick-fix. Done when: rows of all three shapes settle, or
      the verifier no longer writes them, and a re-settle of the affected packets parses every
      row.
      → done 2026-10-21 (branch `claude/practical-cori-e90322`): settle parses the first two
      shapes, and the verifier contract covers the third. In a verify, audit or files row, the
      claim may hold `|`. When the fields after the claim do not fit, it runs to the first `|`
      after which a verdict and a doc reference follow (verify, audit), or a cited section,
      a quote and a verdict (files). A row whose claim holds no `|` splits as before. A files
      row may give `file:first-last`. A verify row's `L<line>` stays one line, since no saved
      report gives a range there. The third shape has no safe parser fix: the debugger line
      never says none, and settle must not read prose as none. So the contract now says that a
      none line is exactly `<section> | none`, beside its rule that no other line holds `|`.
      A micro-test (`claude -p`, Sonnet, no tools, the two packets whose real runs wrote prose,
      5 reps each) found no failure in the control (0/10), against 3 field cases, so it shows
      only that the sentence does no harm. A first wording that offered notes a line of their
      own drew prose notes in 8 of 10 reps and moved a gap from a not-covered row into prose,
      so it was cut. The committed wording gave 0/10 notes and 0/10 unparseable lines. Kit and
      results: `~/.cache/agent-skills/scratch/settle-none-micro/` ($3.30).
      Re-settles parse every row of `hooks/probe-readonly-guard.sh` (5),
      `commands/deferred.md` (8), `build/check_conformance.py`,
      `skills/tune-hyperparameters/SKILL.md` and the round-2 verify. The debugger line still
      holds its file, as intended. `commands/deferred.md` is cited at @2.1.304.3. Its one
      not-covered row is its stamp line, the kind round 3 ruled none as P8.
      `hooks/probe-readonly-guard.sh` stays uncited: plan 58 changed the file after its
      packet was built, so it needs a new files pass. `cli.py lint` prints 6 STALE files.

## 58-claude-code-drift-automation — 2026-10-20
- [x] Two runs of one probe in the same second share a run directory (final reviews: Codex P2,
      and the code-reviewer's Minor M6, which it ranked not worth tracking; the owner chose to
      defer). `probes.run` names the raw-output folder `<cache>/probes/<YYYYmmddTHHMMSS>-<probe id>`,
      and `probes.run_probe` creates it with `exist_ok=True`. Two invocations in one second (two
      worktrees, or a scripted loop) then truncate each other's stream files, and `assess` can read
      the other run's output. Only the log append is locked. Files: `build/cc_guide/probes.py`.
      Size: quick-fix. Done when: the folder is created with a unique suffix (`mkdir(exist_ok=False)`
      with a counter, or `tempfile.mkdtemp` under `probes/`) and a test starts two runs in one second.
      → done 2026-10-21 (/deferred quick fix): `probes.new_run_dir` claims the folder with
      `mkdir` and no `exist_ok`, and adds `-2`, `-3` and so on when a run in the same second
      holds the name. A test runs one probe twice in one second and checks that each folder
      keeps its own stream.
- [x] `held_signals` drops a real stop that follows an ignored signal (final review, Minor M2).
      In `build/cc_guide/probes.py`, `held_signals` records every held signal, even one whose
      previous handler is `SIG_IGN`, and then acts only on the first. A `nohup`-started run that
      gets SIGHUP and then SIGTERM inside one held window acts on neither; in the timeout branch of
      `execute` (up to 10 s) the run then goes on to the next probe and keeps spending. Size:
      quick-fix. Done when: a signal whose previous handler is `SIG_IGN` is not recorded, and a
      process test sends an ignored SIGHUP then SIGTERM and sees the stop acted on once.
      → done 2026-10-21 (/deferred quick fix): `held_signals` leaves a signal whose handler is
      `SIG_IGN` alone, as `term_as_exit` does, so it is neither recorded nor raised. A child
      Python that ignores SIGHUP gets SIGHUP then SIGTERM in a held window, and its SIGTERM
      handler runs once.
- [x] A missing transcript field reads DIVERGES, not ERROR (final review, Minor). In
      `build/cc_guide/probe_checks.py`, `assess_agent_model_pins` reports a forwarded message with no
      `model`, or a code-reviewer transcript with no `effort`, as DIVERGES ("served None", "effort
      None"). Drift spec R10.5 reserves DIVERGES for behaviour that differs from the guide, and ERROR
      for a broken mechanism. The subagent transcript layout is undocumented (a coverage gap), so a
      field rename would send the owner on a paid `verify` run. Size: quick-fix. Done when: a `None`
      model or effort is ERROR with a finding that names the field, and `test_probe_checks.py` pins it.
      Revisit if: Claude Code documents the subagent transcript format.
      → done 2026-10-21 (/deferred quick fix): a forwarded message with no `model` is ERROR
      ("<agent> forwarded a message with no model field"), and so is a code-reviewer transcript
      line with no `effort` ("a code-reviewer transcript line has no effort field"), checked
      before any DIVERGES. `test_probe_checks.py` pins both findings.
- [x] A log row after a blank line is silently skipped (final review, Minor). In
      `build/cc_guide/state.py`, `parse_probes` resets the header on any non-table line, so the
      next `|` line is read as a new header and skipped as "another table". A hand edit or a merge
      resolution that splits `build/cc_guide/PROBES.md` hides later rows, and a DIVERGES row among
      them leaves the probe not due while a PASS is under `probe_days` old. Only
      `test_probe_replay.py`'s check of the committed file guards this, and the commit lints do not
      run that suite. Size: quick-fix. Done when: `parse_probes` raises `SetupError` for a log-table
      data row it would otherwise skip, with a test; or the cc_guide suite joins the commit lints.
      → done 2026-10-21 (/deferred quick fix): a table opens only with a header and a
      separator row, as in Markdown, so a `|` line that opens a block without one raises
      `SetupError` ("row … stands outside a table, so it would be skipped"). A test cuts the log
      table with a blank line, prose and a merge marker. The committed log still parses.
- [x] `hook_response.hook_name` may carry an MCP name into a fixture (final review, Minor).
      `probe_checks._trim_event` keeps `hook_name` verbatim, so a PreToolUse hook with an `mcp__`
      matcher in the owner's user settings would appear as `PreToolUse:mcp__...` in a fixture. Only
      `PRIVATE_RE` in `build/cc_guide/test_probe_replay.py` backstops it, and that suite is not a
      commit lint. Size: quick-fix. Revisit if: a probe's recording shows a hook name with `mcp__`,
      or the owner wires an MCP-matched hook in user settings. Done when: `_trim_event` passes the
      name through the same `mcp__` replacement as tool names.
      → done 2026-10-21 (/deferred quick fix): `MCP_TOKEN_RE` moved from `probes.py` to
      `probe_checks.py`, and `_trim_event` replaces each `mcp__` token in `hook_name` with
      `<mcp-tool>`, as the log row does, so `PreToolUse:mcp__x__y` becomes
      `PreToolUse:<mcp-tool>`. A scrub test pins it.
- [ ] Review Minors from the per-task and final reviews of plan 58, one batch (the owner chose to
      defer). In `build/cc_guide/state.py`: (a) the log-table header test is written twice
      (`is_probe_header` and `parse_probes`'s `PROBE_COLUMNS <= set(header)`); (b) the empty-probe
      branch of `parse_probes` has no test. In `build/cc_guide/probe_checks.py`: (c) `scrub_events`
      replaces private strings after `json.dumps`, so a value with a quote or backslash would not
      match; (d) the dict check in `parse_stream` cannot run; (e) no test for the `None in results`
      branch of `assess_guard_liveness`, the `Task` alias in `DISPATCH_TOOLS`, or scrubbing a
      JSON-escaped value; (f) `broken()` keys only on `is_error`; (g) the MCP token regex in
      `probes.py` swallows a trailing comma, semicolon or colon (appearance only); (h) the Binary
      cell of a log row shows `~` only for the home path as given, not the resolved home or the
      login. In `build/cc_guide/probes.py`: (i) `run_probe` creates `run_dir` and the scratch
      directory before its `try`, so an `OSError` there aborts with a traceback instead of an ERROR
      row; (j) the `stop_group` docstring overstates the grace, and nothing sweeps the process group
      after a normal exit; (k) no test for the scratch-directory-inside-a-git-work-tree ERROR path,
      or for scratch removal on the ERROR and timeout paths; (l) the file has five jobs in about 550
      lines (process control, probe definitions, fixtures, the log, binary discovery), so split
      process control out if it grows; (m) `probes.run` prints the unscrubbed finding to the
      terminal (it never reaches the repo); (n) the three-line private-string test setup is
      repeated in `test_probes.py` between `planted_privates` and the `save_fixtures` test. In
      `build/cc_guide/test_cli.py`: (o) `test_probes_needs_claude_and_takes_only_or_force_not_both`
      takes an unused `capsys` and both cases pass before the subcommand exists; (p) no test of the
      `~` expansion of `--claude` or of the success path of `run_binaries`; (q) `main` computes a
      cache for `binaries`, which does not use it. In `build/cc_guide/test_probe_replay.py`: (r) the
      DIVERGES cases do not pin their finding; (s) no replay case for a changed code-reviewer effort,
      the test-runner model, the control half of the agent-tools DIVERGES, the stop-continuation
      exit-code ERROR, or guard check 1 denied; (t) `PRIVATE_RE` has no `msg_` or `req_` pattern;
      (u) the test reaches `dispatches` through `probes.probe_checks`; (v) one fixture keeps a random
      scratch-folder suffix and the fixtures keep Bash command text for probes that never read it;
      (w) `home` and `home.resolve()` are the same string under `isolated_home`, so dropping one
      `private` entry goes unseen. In `.claude/skills/cc-guide/SKILL.md` and `build/CLAUDE.md`: (x)
      the `probes` mode says "State the count first" but has no way to learn the count (no dry run;
      the due list is the `probes:` line of `cli.py check --worktree`); (y) Ground rules and "After
      any mode" still describe guide edits and settle for every mode; (z) the mode never says that
      exit 1 means a probe DIVERGED or ERRORed; (aa) the `build/CLAUDE.md` probes paragraph is one
      hard-wrapped block with "Nothing is committed for you." after it. See
      `specs/plans/completed/58-claude-code-drift-automation.md` and its Deviation notes. Size: plan. Revisit if: a later edit to `build/cc_guide/probes.py`, `probe_checks.py`,
      `state.py` or one of the test modules above touches one of them. Done when: each is fixed, or
      the owner rules here that it stays.
- [ ] The Stop-hook block cap probe (drift spec, Out of scope; logged at plan completion, as the
      spec's heading says). Size: plan. Revisit if: drift #2 (the Stop-hook continuation reading) is
      resolved toward re-checking.
- [ ] Probes for behavioural claims the repo does not depend on, such as the fork-mode and
      `background` defaults (drift spec, Out of scope). Size: plan. Revisit if: a skill, agent or hook

- [ ] Line-level citations, and anchors for single rows of the guide's tables (drift spec, Out of

- [ ] Widening `build/check_frontmatter.py` to `.claude/skills` and `.claude/agents` (drift spec, Out

- [ ] Hold, owner-only: remove plan 58's scratch files once the branch has merged (plan completion

## cc-guide-files-2026-10-21 (no plan; /cc-guide files, lowmason/agent-skills#94 and branch claude/cc-guide-hooks-files-2.1.306) — 2026-10-21

- [ ] Two branches can give a section's `changed` and a citing file's stamp the same release,
      and `cli.py lint` then calls the file fresh though no verifier read the newer text. On
      2026-10-21, #93 ran `baseline accept --substantive` for `hooks.configuration` on its branch.
      No citer there carried a 2.1.306 stamp, so `next_changed` set `changed` to 2.1.306. On
      another branch, #94 stamped `build/cc_guide/notice.sh` @2.1.306 after a review against the
      guide before #93's line 328 edits. After both merged, the stamp equalled `changed`, so lint
      did not list the file as STALE. R2.4's rule that `accept` stays above every citer's stamp
      reads only the stamps on its own branch (`baseline.accept`, `citer_stamps`). The second
      files pass on branch claude/cc-guide-hooks-files-2.1.306 reviewed `notice.sh` against #93's
      text, so nothing is missed now. Options: record each cited section's `text_hash` in the
      citation or beside it at `cite` time and let lint compare hashes; or a merge-time check
      that flags a section whose `changed` equals a stamp that another merged branch wrote.
      Files: `build/cc_guide/baseline.py`, `build/cc_guide/lint.py`, drift spec R2.4. Size:
      design. Done when: the owner picks a remedy and a test shows that two such branches,
      merged in either order, leave the file STALE, or the owner records the gap as accepted.
- [x] Each probe run inherits the caller's whole environment (`probes.execute` passes
      `env={**os.environ, **inv.env}`). The guide says `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` outranks an
      agent's `effort` field and a per-spawn effort (subagents.frontmatter, guide line 184). So
      if that variable is set in the shell that runs `cli.py probes`, the `agent-model-pins`
      probe reads the variable's effort. Any other value can report DIVERGES for
      code-reviewer's `xhigh` pin, and `xhigh` itself lets the probe pass when the pin is
      broken. Found by the second 2026-10-21 files pass (packet `20261021T110556-files`,
      `build/cc_guide/probes.py:90`), owner ruling R19. Remedy: clear or record that variable
      in the pins run, test-first. Files: `build/cc_guide/probes.py` and its tests. Size:
      quick-fix. Done when: a test shows that the pins run ignores an inherited
      `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL`, or that its row records it.
      → done 2026-10-21 (/deferred quick fix): `execute` drops every name in the new
      `SCRUBBED_ENV` (today only `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL`) from the caller's environment before
      it adds the invocation's own, so every probe run goes without the override, the pins run
      included. `test_a_run_never_inherits_the_effort_override` in `test_probes.py` sets the
      variable, runs a script through `execute` and sees it unset, while a variable the
      invocation adds still arrives. It failed with `low` before the fix. The agent `model`
      field needs no guard against `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, which it outranks. The alias
      remaps (`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL` and its siblings) are a separate item, in section
      deferred-2026-10-21.

## 61-hook-template-fixes — 2026-10-21
- [ ] `hooks/uv-guard.sh` lets through more command shapes than `hooks/README.md` names (plan 61
      final review, Minor; the owner chose to defer). Under `/bin/bash` these exit 0: a command
      after a shell keyword (`if true; then python x.py; fi`, `for f in *; do pytest $f; done`),
      a prefix word given as a path (`/usr/bin/env python x.py`, `/usr/bin/time pytest -q`), a
      backslash escape (`\python x.py`) and the free-threaded name `python3.13t`. The prefix words
      match by exact word, while the final command word matches by its last path part, so the
      header comment "A path counts by its last part" can mislead. The README text and plan 61's
      defaults P3 and P4 fix today's behaviour, so a change needs the owner's go-ahead. For each
      shape, either close it (add `then`, `do`, `else`, `elif` and a last-path-part match to the
      skip set, or widen `python_re`) or name it in `hooks/README.md` and in `DOCUMENTED_GAPS` in
      `hooks/test_project_hooks.py`. See specs/plans/completed/61-hook-template-fixes.md. Size:
      design. Done when: each of the four shapes is closed in the script with a test, or is named
      in the README and pinned in `DOCUMENTED_GAPS`.
- [ ] `hooks/uv-guard.sh` splits a command on `&& || ; | &` without reading quotes or redirects
      (plan 61 final review, Minor, older than plan 61; the owner chose to defer). Three
      effects: `python x.py 2>&1 | tee log` suggests `uv run python x.py 2>` (the `&` of `2>&1`
      splits it), a trailing `&` leaves a trailing space in the suggestion, and
      `echo "a; python x.py"` is a false-positive block (`no-uv-guard` is the escape). Block and
      allow results stay right for common commands. Plan 61's Global Constraints keep the README
      sentence "It splits on `&& || ; | &`", so a fix must change that sentence, with the owner's
      go-ahead. Size: design. Done when: a split that reads quotes and `>&` lands with tests that
      pass under bash 3.2, or the README names these three effects as limits.
- [x] Small cleanups from plan 61's reviews, one batch (the owner chose to defer). (a)
      `hooks/README.md` (the "Tests" section) and the second `hooks` note in `build/suites.toml`
      say the tests skip where `jq` is missing. The `ruff_shim` tests also skip where `/usr/bin`
      or `/bin` holds a `uv`, and the `system-bash` runs skip where `/bin/bash` is absent.
      (b) The install-check text in `hooks/README.md` ("If the transcript shows "ruff
      unavailable" instead ...") starts a sentence with "Then", which reads as a time order, and
      its cause list leaves out a bad ruff config (exit 2). (c) `hooks/ruff-check.sh` cites
      `hooks.events hooks.exit-codes hooks.patterns`. Its comment calls exit 1 a non-blocking
      error, and for a `Stop` hook that also rests on `hooks.pitfalls` (`onFailure: "block"` has
      no effect on `Stop`). Add `hooks.pitfalls`, then restamp with
      `uv run --python 3.13 python build/cc_guide/cli.py baseline cite hooks/ruff-check.sh`.
      (d) `child_env` in `hooks/test_project_hooks.py` passes the caller's whole environment to
      the hook; drop `BASH_ENV` and `SHELLOPTS` so an exported value cannot change the child
      bash. Run it after plan 61 merges: it edits files that plan changes. Size: quick-fix. Done
      when: the four edits land and the hooks suite passes on both interpreters.
      → done 2026-10-21 (quick fix, branch claude/deferred-unblocked-3): (a) both texts now
      name all three skips; (b) the install check lists the cause for each exit status
      (127, 1, 2) and no sentence starts with "Then"; (c) the citation adds `hooks.pitfalls`,
      and its stamp stayed at 2.1.306; (d) `child_env` drops the names in `SCRUBBED_ENV`
      (`BASH_ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`). `test_the_caller_environment_cannot_change_the_hook_bash`
      failed 4 of 4 runs before the fix. The hooks suite passes 400 on both interpreters.
- [ ] Hold, owner-only: check whether the Claude Desktop app shows the first stderr line of a hook
      that exits 1 (plan 61's default P1, not verified). `hooks/ruff-check.sh` exits 1 with
      "ruff-check: ruff unavailable (exit N), so lint was not checked." when ruff cannot run, and
      the install check in `hooks/README.md` tells the reader to look for that line in the
      transcript. The guide says an exit-1 hook's first stderr line shows in the transcript
      (`hooks.exit-codes`); only the CLI was considered, and the Desktop app was not tested. To
      do: in a Desktop session, in a repo where `uv` is not on the hook's `PATH`, end a turn with
      `ruff-check.sh` installed as a `Stop` hook and look for the line. Size: quick-fix. Done
      when: the result is recorded in `hooks/README.md`'s install check (the line shows, or the
      README names the Desktop gap and a different signal).

## 59-model-skill-comparator-and-cv-fixes — 2026-10-21

Plan 59 ticked the audit-3-10-26 items G1, G2, G3 (guards half) and G4. The items below are what its
per-task reviews and its final review (an Opus code-reviewer and a Codex run) left unfixed, triaged
defer. Anchors are at the end of plan 59's fix wave.

- [x] Harden `skills/track-model-experiments/scripts/compare_experiments.py` after plan 59's final
      review. (a) `_cells` strips every outer pipe with `.strip('|')`, so a last cell that ends in
      `\|` right against the closing pipe loses its escape, and a row written `|| b |` loses its
      empty first cell. Remove exactly one outer pipe on each side. (b) The over-wide-row backstop
      in `update_ledger` skips the row. If that row is the top-ranked variant, no row gets the best
      marker. If it holds a stale `**best**`, the ledger can show two. Make the note say so, or
      pick a rule. (c) A ranked winner with no Variants-table row clears every marker with no
      note: name the ranked ids that the table lacks. (d) `main()` reads `--ledger` before it
      writes `--output`, so a mistyped ledger path gives a `FileNotFoundError` traceback and no
      `comparison.json`: check `args.ledger.is_file()` first and exit through `error:`. (e) The
      metric-column lookup is case-sensitive, so a header such as `elpd` gets no fill and no
      message: print one note when no column matched. (f) Test gaps in
      `test_compare_experiments.py`: the winner row's cells and marker; the `None` divergence
      rendering; the `count == 0` branch of `pointwise_count`; a row shorter than the header; a
      Variants header with no `status`; an unterminated `COMPARISON:BEGIN`; the over-wide-row test
      should assert that the other rows still get their marker and metric cells; `_row` reads the
      first line that starts with the id, so it depends on the block sitting below the table; the
      escaped-pipe asserts use double-quoted f-strings, where the file uses single quotes, and the
      helper `_unescaped_cells` copies `_cells`' regex. Size: plan. Done when: (a) to (e) are fixed
      or dropped on purpose, each with a test, and the (f) gaps are filled. → done in plan 65
- [ ] Three nits in the splitter's tests (plan 59 final review).

      gap (the acceptance run showed exact gaps); `test_one_row_per_period_matches_row_mode` sends
      both paths through `_period_index`, so the baseline tests are what pin row mode; a 0-d
      `groups` raises `IndexError` at `labels.shape[0]`, not the `ValueError` that the length check
      gives. Size: quick-fix. Revisit if: someone edits `time_series_cv.py` again, or a panel run
      shows a leak.

## 65-model-lifecycle — 2026-10-22

Plan 65 (model-lifecycle Stage 1) ticked the comparator-hardening item and part (a) of the doc-nits
item. The items below are what its per-task reviews and its final review (an Opus code-reviewer and a
Codex run) left unfixed, triaged defer. Both plan-mandated findings the owner chose to fix were fixed
(commit `caad7c6`). Anchors are at the end of plan 65.

- [ ] Comparator ledger-guard nits (plan 65 Task 1 review). (a) `ledger_problem` counts a marker as a
      substring anywhere in the text, while `_variants_table` matches a marker only as a whole stripped
      line, so a prose mention of a marker next to a real pair is refused, and a mid-line BEGIN passes.
      (b) The marker guard lives only in `main()`, so a library caller of `update_ledger` can still
      rewrite a malformed ledger. (c) The marker-pair test lacks the lone-END (0,1) and BEGIN-BEGIN-END
      (2,1) cases. (d) The empty-analysis-dir marker test is meaningful only through its
      `'one pair of markers'` assert: rebuild it on two `scores.json` variants plus a score ledger.
      In `skills/track-model-experiments/scripts/compare_experiments.py` and its test file. Size:
      quick-fix. Revisit if: `update_ledger` gains a caller outside `main()`, or a ledger loses prose.
- [x] Comparator robustness (plan 65 Task 2 and final reviews). (a) A huge JSON integer or a
      near-1e308 float makes `math.isfinite` or `statistics.fmean` raise `OverflowError`, which skips
      the `ValueError` handling in `load_scores` and `compare_scores` and prints a traceback instead of
      `error:`. (b) Two `--folders` paths with the same basename overwrite each other in the dict, as
      the ELPD path also does. (c) `update_ledger` writes with a plain `write_text`, not a temp file and
      rename. (d) `load_scores` refusals with no test: invalid JSON, a non-object, a missing
      criterion, a bad orientation, `units` that is not an object, string or bool values (one
      parametrized test covers them). Size: quick-fix. Done when: (a) and (b) fail through `error:`,
      or are dropped on purpose, and (d) has a test.
      → done 2026-10-28 (/deferred quick fix): (a) `load_scores` now refuses a score above
      `MAX_SCORE` (1e300) in magnitude, which covers the 400-digit integer and the near-1e308
      float and keeps sums and paired differences finite. It fails through `error:`. (b)
      `check_distinct_names` refuses two `--folders` paths with one name in both modes. (c)
      dropped on purpose: a temp file and rename would swap a symlinked ledger for a plain file
      and reset its mode, and a crash during the write leaves a ledger that git restores. (d)
      ten parametrized cases in `test_compare_experiments.py`. Every new test that targets a fix
      failed before the edit.
- [x] Comparator contract gaps (plan 65 Task 2 and final reviews). (a) A `scores.json` that declares
      `criterion: elpd-loo` can never match a score ledger, because a ledger that declares `elpd-loo`
      forces ELPD mode. `predictive-evaluation.md` now says so in one sentence. Decide whether
      `load_scores` should refuse the name. (b) `comparison.json` carries `mode` and `criterion` but
      not `orientation`, so a consumer reads the direction from the sign of Δscore. (c) `mode_problem`
      does not say that the `Criterion:` line must sit above the Variants table, so a user who put it
      below sees "reads as elpd-loo". Size: quick-fix. Done when: each of (a) to (c) is fixed or
      dropped on purpose.
      → done 2026-10-28 (/deferred quick fix): (a) fixed: `load_scores` refuses the criterion
      `elpd-loo` and names `lfo-elpd` and `cv-logloss` as usable names, so the error comes from
      the `scores.json` and not from a ledger mismatch two edits later. (b) fixed:
      `comparison.json` carries `orientation` (`higher` for every ELPD run). (c) fixed: when a
      ledger reads as `elpd-loo`, `mode_problem` says that a `Criterion:` line counts only above
      the Variants table and outside the COMPARISON block. `SKILL.md` and
      `references/predictive-evaluation.md` record all three.
- [x] Track doc polish (plan 65 reviews). (a) `skills/track-model-experiments/SKILL.md`'s When-to-use
      re-declare step does not say to swap the ELPD columns for `score`, `Δscore` and `SE(Δ)`, so stale
      ELPD cells stay and only a stderr note warns. (b) Two sections still assume ELPD and
      bayesian-workflow: "Each variant wraps a full `bayesian-workflow` … loop" and selection-induced
      bias "by ELPD". Both apply to score-mode ledgers too. (c) The `elpd-loo` sentence in
      `references/predictive-evaluation.md` is dense: split it in two. (d) The V1 record
      `specs/verification/65-track-description-microtest.md` could say that the init record's `skills`
      array is metadata only (about 5,900 cache-creation tokens rule out a leaked listing). (e) The
      new description drops the old trigger "comparing many NumPyro/ArviZ model versions" (R20 widened
      it on purpose, and the ELPD prompt T3 still routed 5/5); restore it if a routing miss shows.
      Size: quick-fix. Done when: (a) to (d) are edited, and (e) is either restored or dropped on
      purpose.
      → done 2026-10-28 (/deferred quick fix): (a) to (d) edited. (a) the re-declare step in
      `SKILL.md` now says to swap the ELPD columns for `score`, `Δscore` and `SE(Δ)`. (b) the
      fresh-session section and the selection-bias paragraph cover score ledgers. (c) the
      `elpd-loo` sentence in `predictive-evaluation.md` is split in two. (d) the V1 record says

## 69-agent-payload-probe — 2026-10-28
- [ ] The `agent-payload` probe cannot tell a parent session that disobeyed its prompt from real drift
      (plan 69 final review: the Codex P2 and the code-reviewer's Minors 1 and 2, one defect). In
      `check_payloads` in `build/cc_guide/probe_checks.py`, the control check says the `general-purpose`
      dispatch "passed model 'haiku'", and the built-in check says a dispatch was "sent with no model".
      The code reads neither fact from the stream's `tool_use` inputs. If the parent leaves `haiku` off the
      control, or gives `claude-code-guide` a model, the probe reports DIVERGES. Plan 69's live run had a human
      check of the parent's Agent calls (Task 4 Step 3), and later `/cc-guide probes` runs have none. That live
      run was sound: its parent sent `haiku` to the control and no model to `claude-code-guide`. The same
      function returns the ERROR "the PreToolUse hooks logged no subagent dispatch" when the hooks logged
      payloads and `trim_payload` dropped them all (a renamed `subagent_type`, a moved `tool_input`, or
      main-session payloads that carry `agent_id`). To fix it: make `_trim_block`
      (`build/cc_guide/probe_checks.py`, which open items of the plan 58 Minors batch, section
      `58-claude-code-drift-automation — 2026-10-20`, also cover) keep `model` in the parent's `tool_use`
      inputs, give `assess_agent_payload` a check that returns ERROR when those inputs differ from what the
      prompt asked for, count the raw payloads before trimming, re-save the fixtures from the raw run, and add
      replay cases. See `specs/plans/completed/69-agent-payload-probe.md` (D6). Size: plan. Done when: a parent
      that skips the requested model gives ERROR, not DIVERGES, with a replay case that proves it, and the
      empty-log ERROR names the stage that dropped the payloads.
- [ ] Review Minors from plan 69's final review (code, triaged defer). `first_by_type` in
      `build/cc_guide/probe_checks.py` takes the first payload of a type, but `collect_payload` in
      `build/cc_guide/probes.py` sorts the payload files by a random `mktemp` suffix, so "first" is arbitrary
      when the parent dispatches one type twice with different inputs. The control-model check in
      `check_payloads` also fires if Claude Code starts to normalise `'haiku'` to a full model id, which
      `decide()` in `hooks/dispatch-model-guard.py` would not mind. `check_payloads` reads the model from the
      `*`-matcher payload, not from the `Agent`-matcher payload that the installed guard receives.
      `first_by_type` is annotated `dict[str, dict]`, but its key can be `None`. `assess_agent_payload`
      compares the served model sets for equality, so a control served by two Haiku ids would flag a matching
      built-in. The `bad_payload` test in `build/cc_guide/test_probes.py` plants its bad file only in the `any`
      folder. The string `'Agent'` appears three times in `probe_checks.py` (`DISPATCH_TOOLS[0]`,
      `GUARDED_TOOL`, `GUARD_MATCHER`), and the guard-constants test ties only two. See
      `specs/plans/completed/69-agent-payload-probe.md` (D8). Size: quick-fix. Revisit if: a real run gives a
      false DIVERGES or ERROR that traces to one of these, or the first item of this section lands (then fix
      these in the same change).

## spec-reading-test-check (no plan; owner-approved local design idea from session "Obra superpowers writing-plans mechanism") — 2026-10-28
- [ ] Spec-reading test check (local design, not an upstream finding, owner-approved
      2026-10-28): no step from writing-plans through subagent-driven-development checks the
      plan's test assertions against the spec. The planner writes the tests and the code
      (`skills/writing-plans/SKILL.md:134-152`, enforced by `:167-181`). Its self-review
      (`:185-193`) checks that each spec requirement has a task, not that the assertions match
      the spec. The task reviewer compares the diff to the brief (`agents/task-reviewer.md:87-89`).
      The final code-reviewer gets the plan, not the spec (`skills/executing-plans/SKILL.md:65-66`).
      The Codex seat never sees the plan (`skills/requesting-code-review/codex-review.md:5-6`).
      A planner's misread therefore passes every gate, and only the owner's plan review can
      catch it. Superpowers drift findings 9 and 10 do not close it. Neither does upstream
      v7.0.1's pre-flight scan (finding 7). It checks a task's tests against that task's
      code, so a misread that test and code share passes it. Five layers, in the order a
      misread can enter:
      (1) Spec: owner-approved worked examples with exact values (inputs, outputs,
      boundaries). They are the only reference that no model produced. This lands in
      brainstorming, so coordinate with the brainstorming rebuild.
      (2) Plan: adopt findings 9 and 10 as the base. They separate the code from the tests,
      but not one reading of the spec from another.
      (3) Plan review: Codex writes acceptance tests from the spec and the plan's Interfaces
      blocks only, never from the plan's tests or approach lines. The owner gets a table of
      the disagreements (spec quote, plan assertion, Codex assertion). Each ruling becomes a
      `## Decisions` entry, or a spec-amendment task when the spec was unclear.
      (4) Execution: the implementer brief carries the relevant spec excerpts. The implementer
      reports a conflict between a test and the spec as DONE_WITH_CONCERNS, and never edits a
      plan test to fit its own reading.
      (5) End of run: before the final review, run the reconciled acceptance tests, which the
      implementer never saw. Triage each failure as code wrong, acceptance test wrong, or spec
      unclear (the owner rules). Pass the spec path to code-reviewer with the plan. That part
      is a quick fix that can land alone.
      Rules: no agent resolves a disagreement between two readings. Only the owner does. Add no
      further Claude reviewers that start from the plan. Mutation testing is out of scope,
      because it measures test strength, not correctness.
      Probes before building: take two or three specs and plans from `specs/completed/` and
      `specs/plans/completed/`. Plant misreads in the plans (an inclusive bound made exclusive,
      a wrong unit, a wrong default), and check that the current pipeline misses them. That
      is the failing baseline. Then add each layer, and keep only the layers that catch
      something. The design expects Codex to share fewer errors with the planner, but nobody
      has measured that, and the probes measure it. Codex runs send the spec and the plan to
      OpenAI, so a private project needs owner approval beyond this repository. Sequence: after
      the owner decides findings 9 and 10. Size: design. Done when: a brainstorming spec
      decides the layers and records the baseline probe results.

## 72-writing-skills-split — 2026-10-29
- [x] `build/check_links.py` fails open on forms it does not parse (plan 72 final review, Minor;
      triaged defer). A misspelled directory argument (`check_links.py skils`) prints
      `0 relative link(s) checked, 0 broken` and exits 0, because `rglob` on a missing directory
      yields nothing. A link title in single quotes or in parentheses (`[t](gone.md 'title')`)
      does not match `LINK`, so the link is never checked. On a case-insensitive file system such
      as this Mac's, `exists()` accepts a link whose case differs from the file name, while Linux
      CI would reject it. Reference-style definitions (`[ref]: path.md`) and percent-encoded
      targets are not handled either. No such link exists in `skills/` today: 213 links checked,
      0 broken, and a case-sensitive pass is also clean. Indented code blocks and code spans that
      wrap across lines can only cause false alarms. See
      `specs/plans/completed/72-writing-skills-split.md`. Size: quick-fix. Done when:
      `check_links.py` exits non-zero for a named directory that does not exist or when it checks
      zero links, counts links with single-quoted or parenthesized titles, compares file names
      case-sensitively, and parses reference-style definitions and percent-encoded targets, and
      `build/test_check_links.py` has a test for each.
      → done 2026-10-29 (/deferred quick fix): test-first, seven new tests in
      `build/test_check_links.py`. A missing named directory exits 2, and zero links checked
      exits 1. Footnote definitions stay skipped. The real tree still gives 213 links, 0 broken.

      `specs/red-baseline-writing-skills-split/kit/run_reps.sh`, copied from plan 71's kit
      (`specs/red-baseline-sdd-compaction/kit/run_reps.sh`), does not isolate the reps from the
      user's output style, hooks or user-level CLAUDE.md. The cause is not identified. That
      CLAUDE.md tells agents to check for a relevant skill, so the `t3` read-first passes are an
      upper bound. The same leak may touch plan 71's results. The kit also makes one

      Insight box and no quoted user-level file in any reply, plan 72's `t3` arms (30 reps, about
